# Evidence ledger: sponsor_strict_2026

- Question: Estimate the number of distinct job postings published in the United States during calendar year 2026 (2026-01-01 to 2026-12-31) for new-graduate roles — summer internships plus full-time entry-level positions for new bachelor's or master's graduates with 0–1 years of experience, in professional (white-collar) occupations — whose posting text explicitly states that the employer will sponsor a work visa (for example "H-1B sponsorship available" or "visa sponsorship provided"). Count each posting once even if it appears on several job boards. Do not count postings that do not state sponsorship in their text, even if the employer has sponsored visas before or ticks a "will sponsor" box on a job platform, and do not count postings that rule sponsorship out.
- as_of: 2026-10-28
- Pinned target: Distinct U.S. job postings first published in calendar year 2026 for new-graduate roles — summer internships plus full-time entry-level positions for new bachelor's or master's graduates with 0–1 years of experience — in professional/white-collar occupations, whose posting text explicitly states tha | unit distinct job posting | period 2026-01-01..2026-12-31 | publisher No official publisher; analyst-constructed estimate from job boards, employer career sites, administrative immigration data, and hiring surveys.

## Directions and pages

### d1 [related_series] job-board/labor-market data aggregator

Hypothesis: The target can be estimated top-down as total U.S. professional white-collar unique postings first published in 2026 multiplied by the share for new bachelor's/master's 0–1 years or summer internships and by the share explicitly stating visa sponsorship. The first two factors set the at-risk universe; if professional postings or new-grad hiring rebound, the target rises. Transfer assumption: job-board experience-level and occupation tags approximate the question's role definitions.

Queries issued by the searcher: Indeed Hiring Lab US job postings 2026 index; Lightcast job postings data 2026 occupation experience level; LinkUp total unique job postings per month United States 2026; share of job postings mentioning visa sponsorship percent study; Indeed Hiring Lab US job postings 2026 index; Lightcast job postings data 2026 occupation experience level; share of job postings mentioning visa sponsorship percentage research; LinkUp job postings count 2026 total unique postings per month; Indeed Hiring Lab US job postings 2026 index; Lightcast job postings data 2026 occupation experience level; new graduate job postings share professional occupations 2026; Lightcast "unique job postings" per month United States 2026 total count; share of job postings mention visa sponsorship H-1B percent Lightcast 2026; Handshake Class of 2026 graduation report job postings down percent

### d2 [base_rates] job-board search/count analyst

Hypothesis: The target is highly sensitive to the share of new-grad professional postings whose text contains explicit sponsorship phrases. Measuring phrase prevalence on major boards for 2026 gives a direct multiplier on the at-risk universe. If sponsorship language is rare and falling, the target falls; if employers increasingly advertise sponsorship to attract international students, the target rises. Transfer assumption: sampled boards and occupations represent all U.S. new-grad professional postings after deduplication.

Queries issued by the searcher: share of job postings mentioning visa sponsorship percentage study; "visa sponsorship" job postings analysis percentage of postings Indeed LinkedIn study; Handshake data share of full-time job postings offering visa sponsorship 2023 10.9% 2026 2.6%; Handshake report international students sponsorship job postings decline 2026; share of job postings mentioning visa sponsorship LinkedIn Indeed study; "visa sponsorship" job postings percentage analysis 2025; Handshake visa sponsorship job postings share 2026 report international students; Bloomberg international students H-1B sponsorship job postings Handshake 2026; share of job postings mentioning visa sponsorship LinkedIn study percentage; "visa sponsorship" job postings percentage 2025 study H-1B new grad; Handshake number of job postings 2026 internships entry-level postings total students; "entry level" job postings 2026 count total US new graduate hiring report LinkedIn Indeed

### d3 [administrative] immigration-regulator data analyst

Hypothesis: Employers that actually sponsor H-1B visas form a constrained set; LCA and H-1B employer/occupation data identify likely sponsors and their entry-level hiring occupations. Target postings should scale with cap-subject registrations and LCA filings for entry-level wage levels, but only where employers explicitly advertise sponsorship. If administrative sponsorship volume rises in FY2026, explicit-sponsorship postings likely rise, with lag and policy effects. Transfer assumption: administrative sponsorship activity maps to employer posting behavior after adjusting for the share that advertises.

Queries issued by the searcher: USCIS H-1B Employer Data Hub FY2025 data; DOL LCA disclosure data 2025 wage level occupation download; FY2026 H-1B cap registration number selected USCIS 2025; share of job postings mentioning visa sponsorship percentage study; USCIS H-1B Employer Data Hub FY2025; DOL LCA disclosure data 2025 wage level occupation download; top H-1B sponsors 2025 list employers approved petitions; H-1B cap registrations FY2026 selected number USCIS; USCIS H-1B Employer Data Hub FY2025 data; DOL LCA disclosure data 2025 wage level occupation; H-1B cap registrations FY2026 USCIS number selected; top H-1B sponsors 2025 list employers LCA filings

### d4 [persona] large employer/campus recruiter

Hypothesis: Summing over large professional employers and known H-1B sponsors, target postings equal their 2026 new-grad/internship requisitions that explicitly state sponsorship. Employer career sites show actual requisition counts and exact text; if top sponsors expand campus hiring and advertise sponsorship, the target rises. Transfer assumption: sampled employers represent the sponsor-advertising segment after coverage adjustment.

Queries issued by the searcher: "visa sponsorship" "new graduate" 2026 careers site job postings; top H-1B sponsors 2025 list employers USCIS; Lightcast job postings 2026 entry-level "new graduate" count analysis; Handshake 2026 internship job postings number employers campus recruiting report; "H-1B sponsorship" "new graduate" 2026 careers job postings; campus recruiting 2026 visa sponsorship employer new grad internships; share of U.S. job postings that mention visa sponsorship percent Lightcast study; NACE Job Outlook 2026 employer hiring projections new college graduates percent; top H-1B sponsors 2025 list employers number of petitions; "visa sponsorship" "new graduate" 2026 careers job postings; share of job postings mentioning visa sponsorship percent Lightcast Burning Glass study; NACE Job Outlook 2026 hiring projections bachelor's graduates employers

### d5 [own_history] time-series analyst

Hypothesis: Earlier-year counts of the same phrase-and-role construct, if available, provide a base level and seasonality; 2026 equals the prior-year annualized count adjusted for observed 2026 YTD labor demand, sponsorship policy, and occupation mix. If 2026 YTD runs above or below comparable prior-year months, the full-year target moves in the same direction. Transfer assumption: phrase/role definition and board coverage are consistent over time.

### d6 [forecasts_markets] university/campus hiring market analyst

Hypothesis: New-grad and internship posting volume is constrained by employer campus hiring plans and internship capacity. If NACE or employer surveys project higher 2026 new-grad hires and internship conversion, the at-risk universe of new-grad postings rises; explicit-sponsorship share then scales the target. Transfer assumption: hiring-plan indices and internship counts map proportionally to posting counts, not one-to-one.

Queries issued by the searcher: NACE Job Outlook 2026 new graduate hiring projections; employers plan to hire new graduates 2026 survey percent increase; NACE 2026 internship hiring employers expect to hire interns summer 2026 percent; share of U.S. job postings mentioning visa sponsorship H-1B percent entry-level; NACE Job Outlook 2026 new graduate hiring projections; employers plan to hire new college graduates 2026 survey percent increase; NACE 2026 Internship and Co-op Report interns hiring increase conversion rate; Handshake 2026 job postings entry-level volume report new grad; NACE Job Outlook 2026 new graduate hiring projections; employers plan to hire new college graduates 2026 survey percent increase; NACE 2026 Internship Co-op Report employers plan intern hiring increase percent 2025; share of job postings mention visa sponsorship entry-level H-1B "will not sponsor" percent study

### d7 [shocks] policy/labor-market monitor

Hypothesis: Immigration-policy changes and labor-market shocks can alter both the number of new-grad roles and whether employers state sponsorship. Restrictive H-1B rules or fees may reduce explicit sponsorship language; labor shortages may increase it. The direction of the target depends on measured exposure; these shocks must adjust any historical extrapolation. Transfer assumption: announced policy changes affect 2026 postings with identifiable timing.

Queries issued by the searcher: H-1B policy change 2026 sponsorship job postings; companies stopped sponsoring H-1B 2025 2026; entry-level job postings 2026 new graduate hiring trend Indeed data; Handshake job postings visa sponsorship share decline 2026; H-1B policy change 2026 sponsorship job postings; companies stopped sponsoring H-1B 2025 2026; 2026 tech layoffs entry level hiring; H-1B $100,000 fee proclamation September 2025 USCIS; H-1B policy change 2026 sponsorship job postings; companies stopped sponsoring H-1B 2025 2026; 2026 tech layoffs entry level hiring; H-1B $100,000 fee proclamation September 2025 USCIS

### d8 [related_series] job-board deduplication/coverage analyst

Hypothesis: Platform counts overcount because the same requisition appears on multiple boards and under multiple locations; the target requires unique employer requisitions. Estimating cross-board overlap and board coverage converts platform phrase counts into unique postings. If overlap is high, raw counts overstate the target; if many postings are only on employer sites or niche boards, extrapolation understates. Transfer assumption: overlap and coverage ratios from sampled postings apply to the new-grad sponsorship segment.

Queries issued by the searcher: job posting deduplication across job boards overlap study unique postings; same job posting LinkedIn Indeed duplicate rate study; Lightcast job postings deduplication methodology unique postings number 2025; job board market share unique job postings 2025 Indeed LinkedIn percentage; job posting deduplication across boards overlap study; same job posting LinkedIn Indeed duplicate rate study; job board market share unique job postings 2025; cross-posting duplicate job listings percentage research; job posting deduplication across boards overlap study; same job posting LinkedIn Indeed duplicate rate study; Lightcast job posting analytics methodology unique postings deduplication definition; job board market share 2025 unique postings Indeed LinkedIn percentage

### b1 [base_rates] Direct phrase audit of 2026 new-grad professional postings

Hypothesis: The largest uncertainty is the explicit-sponsorship share for the exact target segment. A direct audit split by internship/full-time, occupation, degree level, and first-publication year would replace assumptions a3, a5, and a6 and resolve the disagreement between direct text samples (roughly 0.1–1.1%) and Handshake's 2.6% early-career platform share.

Queries issued by the searcher: share of job postings mentioning visa sponsorship Handshake new grad report; Lightcast job postings visa sponsorship phrase by occupation; CNBC Handshake visa sponsorship postings 2.6% 2026 international students make it; analysis job postings mentioning "visa sponsorship" less than 1% of postings study; Handshake 2026 visa sponsorship new grad report explicit text; Lightcast job postings visa sponsorship phrase by occupation share; LinkedIn Economic Graph visa sponsorship job postings share; employer ATS text audit new grad visa sponsorship share of postings; Handshake 2026 visa sponsorship new grad report explicit text; Lightcast job postings visa sponsorship phrase by occupation share; share of job postings mentioning visa sponsorship percent new graduate; "will not sponsor" job postings analysis percent entry level H-1B study

### b2 [related_series] Annual unique posting flow by occupation and seniority

Hypothesis: The LinkUp active-stock-times-duration route requires a steady-state duration transfer and an occupation mix assumption. A direct 2026 annual count of distinct first-published U.S. postings, split by professional occupation and 0–1 year/internship seniority, would replace p75/p81 as the denominator and materially tighten m1.

### b3 [base_rates] Employer-side requisition counts at likely sponsors

Hypothesis: The administrative routes depend on assumptions a8–a11 about how many sponsor employers advertise new-grad roles and how many postings per employer. Counting 2026 new-grad/internship requisitions with explicit sponsorship language on the career sites or ATS feeds of top H-1B sponsors and large campus recruiters would calibrate those factors and provide an independent employer-coverage check.

Queries issued by the searcher: share of job postings mentioning visa sponsorship 2024 study; top H-1B sponsor employers 2025 list Amazon Infosys Microsoft number of petitions; F1 Hire International Talent Friendliness Index 2025 percentage job postings sponsorship friendly; NACE job outlook 2026 new graduate hiring job postings entry-level count; top H-1B sponsors 2025 list employers USCIS data hub; share of job postings mentioning visa sponsorship 2025 study; Handshake visa sponsorship share of full-time job postings 2026 report data; Handshake new graduate job postings 2026 class of 2026 hiring report number of postings; site:careers.amazon.jobs 2026 new grad visa sponsorship; Microsoft university careers 2026 visa sponsorship; top H-1B sponsor career site new graduate sponsorship 2026; ATS requisition count new grad visa sponsorship 2026

## Assumptions (proposed by the judge, validated by code)

Rules: ids a1..a12, at most 6 per method, every formula needs an observed point; sampled lognormally with P10/P90 = low/high, widened by code to high/low >= 2.0. From judge round 2.

| id | name | value | judge range | range used (P10..P90) | unit | used by | rationale | applies when | falsified by |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| a1 | Professional white-collar share of all U.S. unique postings | 45 | 30..60 | 30..60 | % | m1 (m2) | Management, business, science, and arts occupations are a large but minority share of all U.S. postings; broad professional/white-collar definitions typically fall in the 35–55% range. The range allows for narrower professional definitions and lower-skill posting mix. | Used to convert all-occupation posting flow into a professional/white-collar universe. | Direct 2026 all-posting occupation tabulation showing professional white-collar share far outside 30–60%. |
| a2 | Full-time new-grad 0–1 year share within professional postings | 10 | 5..20 | 5..20 | % | m1 (m2) | Broad Indeed 'entry-level' is 46%, but the target requires 0–1 years and new bachelor's/master's targeting. A local tabulation finds about 14% of unique postings with minimum experience 0–1, and professional roles skew more senior; 10% is central with a wide range for definitional uncertainty. | Used to isolate full-time 0–1-year professional postings from all professional postings. | A 2026 professional-occupation tabulation showing the 0–1-year new-grad share outside 5–20%. |
| a4 | Summer internship share within professional postings | 6 | 3..12 | 3..12 | % | m1 (m2) | A September 2026 location-screened sample has 984 internship postings out of 11,304 U.S. postings (about 8.7%); professional internships should be somewhat less common than all internships, so 6% is central with a wide range. | Used to isolate professional summer internships from all professional postings. | A direct 2026 professional posting sample showing internship share outside 3–12%. |
| a6 | Relative explicit-sponsorship intensity of new-grad professional postings versus all U.S. postings | 4 | 1.5..10 | 1.5..10 | ratio | m1 (m2) | All-posting explicit sponsorship is about 0.18% in 2026, while software/data/education functions are 0.7–1.11% and physician roles 3.2%; Handshake early-career full-time is 2.6%. New-grad professional roles should be several times the all-posting rate but not uniformly 14x, so 4x is central. | Used to transfer the all-posting JobShifu explicit-sponsorship share to the new-grad professional segment. | Direct 2026 new-grad professional text audit showing sponsorship intensity relative to all postings outside 1.5–10x. |
| a8 | Share of FY2026 H-1B cap-registering employers with at least one qualifying explicitly sponsored new-grad/internship posting | 6 | 2..15 | 2..15 | % | m2 (m3) | Most cap registrants are existing sponsors of experienced workers, staffing/consulting firms, or small employers; only a minority hire new bachelor's/master's graduates and explicitly advertise sponsorship. 61% file a single petition and 95% file ten or fewer, supporting a low share. | Used to convert unique FY2026 H-1B cap-registering employers into employers that advertise qualifying new-grad/internship roles. | Employer-site audit showing a much larger or smaller share of cap registrants with explicit new-grad sponsorship postings. |
| a9 | Average qualifying postings per advertising sponsor employer | 3 | 1..8 | 1..8 | posting per employer | m2 (m3) | Large campus recruiters post multiple new-grad and internship requisitions, while the median sponsor files few petitions and may post one. A mean of about 3 with a wide range reflects this skew. | Used with the advertising-sponsor employer count to estimate distinct qualifying postings. | A 2026 employer-level count of explicit new-grad sponsorship postings showing mean postings per advertising sponsor outside 1–8. |
| a10 | Share of LCA filings for new-grad 0–1 year professional roles | 3 | 1..8 | 1..8 | % | m3 (m4) | Most H-1B LCAs are for experienced or continuing workers; entry-level roles have low lottery selection and are a minority of filings. Three percent is central with a wide range because new-grad professional hiring is concentrated among some sponsors. | Used to isolate the new-grad 0–1-year portion of certified LCAs before converting to advertised postings. | LCA wage-level/occupation tabulation for 2026 showing new-grad 0–1-year share outside 1–8%. |
| a11 | Ratio of distinct 2026 explicit-sponsorship new-grad professional postings to new-grad LCA filings | 0.8 | 0.3..2 | 0.3..2 | posting per filing | m3 (m4) | One advertised posting can lead to multiple LCAs across hires, but many LCAs are never advertised with explicit sponsorship and some postings are internships. A ratio near one with a wide range is appropriate. | Used to convert new-grad LCA volume into distinct advertised postings with explicit sponsorship language. | Matched employer audit showing postings per new-grad LCA far outside 0.3–2.0. |
| a5 | Coverage ratio of LCA-filing sponsor universe: qualifying postings by LCA-filing employers divided by all qualifying postings | 0.8 | 0.5..0.95 | 0.4873..0.9747 (widened) | ratio | m3 (m4) | Most explicit H-1B sponsorship postings come from employers that file LCAs, but cap-exempt universities, green-card-only sponsors, and employers that never actually file still post explicit sponsorship language. A high central coverage ratio with a wide lower tail lets the LCA route represent the fu | Used to expand the LCA-derived count to all qualifying explicit-sponsorship postings, including those from employers outside the LCA-filing universe. | A matched audit showing that LCA filers account for far less than half of explicit new-grad sponsorship postings. |
| a7 | Coverage ratio of cap-registering sponsor universe: qualifying postings by FY2026 cap-registering employers divided by all qualifying postings | 0.7 | 0.4..0.9 | 0.4..0.9 | ratio | m2 (m3) | Cap-registering employers are the main H-1B sponsors but exclude cap-exempt employers and employers that sponsor only green cards or never enter the lottery. The ratio is lower than for the broader LCA universe because cap registration is a narrower subset. | Used to expand the cap-registering-employer route to all qualifying explicit-sponsorship postings. | Employer audit showing cap registrants account for far less than 40% of explicit new-grad sponsorship postings. |
| a12 | Share of all explicit-sponsorship U.S. postings that are new-grad professional roles | 0.25 | 0.1..0.5 | 0.1..0.5 | ratio | m4 (m5) | Explicit sponsorship postings skew toward experienced tech, healthcare, and consulting roles, but early-career full-time sponsorship is also present (Handshake 2.6% vs all-posting 0.18%). A quarter of explicit-sponsorship postings being new-grad professional is central with a wide range. | Used to convert the annualized all-occupation explicit-sponsorship posting flow into the new-grad professional target. | A direct text audit of explicit-sponsorship postings showing the new-grad professional share outside 10–50%. |

## Methods (computed by code)

| id | method | estimate | inputs (points / assumptions) | backtest years | mean abs ln err | weight | weight basis | notes / error |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| m1 | formula_mc | 18,376 | ['p75', 'p81', 'p157'] / ['a1', 'a2', 'a4', 'a6'] | [] |  | 0.00 | B1 of 4 blocks; median of blocks: not the median (one vote, counts in the interval) |  |
| m2 | formula_mc | 9,413 | ['p259'] / ['a7', 'a8', 'a9'] | [] |  | 0.00 | B2 of 4 blocks; median of blocks: not the median (one vote, counts in the interval) |  |
| m3 | formula_mc | 17,194 | ['p368'] / ['a10', 'a11', 'a5'] | [] |  | 0.50 | B3 of 4 blocks; median of blocks: this block is the median |  |
| m4 | formula_mc | 14,554 | ['p154', 'p153', 'p75', 'p81'] / ['a12'] | [] |  | 0.50 | B4 of 4 blocks; median of blocks: this block is the median |  |

- m1 coverage: the whole target
- m2 coverage: the whole target
- m3 coverage: the whole target
- m4 coverage: the whole target

Method details (computed by code):

- m1 formula_mc: (p75 * k_days_per_year / p81) * a1 * (a2 + a4) * p157 * a6; Monte Carlo P10 to P90 5,592 to 59,245; result unit (posting/year) * 1 year converted to posting; own 80% width x/÷3.26 (Monte Carlo of the formula inputs)
- m2 formula_mc: p259 * a8 * a9 / a7; Monte Carlo P10 to P90 2,097 to 42,586; result unit (0.05263157895 posting/month) * 1 year converted to posting; own 80% width x/÷4.51 (Monte Carlo of the formula inputs)
- m3 formula_mc: p368 * a10 * a11 / a5; Monte Carlo P10 to P90 3,943 to 73,945; result unit (posting/year) * 1 year converted to posting; own 80% width x/÷4.33 (Monte Carlo of the formula inputs)
- m4 formula_mc: (p154 * p75 / p153) * (k_days_per_year / p81) * a12; Monte Carlo P10 to P90 5,985 to 34,893; result unit (posting/year) * 1 year converted to posting; own 80% width x/÷2.41 (Monte Carlo of the formula inputs)
- Combination: 4 block(s), equal weight per block (methods whose inputs overlap by at least half form one block; backtest errors set widths, not weights): B1 = m1: 18,376 (width x/÷3.26, Monte Carlo of the formula inputs); B2 = m2: 9,413 (width x/÷4.51, Monte Carlo of the formula inputs); B3 = m3: 17,194 (width x/÷4.33, Monte Carlo of the formula inputs); B4 = m4: 14,554 (width x/÷2.41, Monte Carlo of the formula inputs). Estimate = the median of the blocks.
- Interval: root mean square of the block widths x/÷3.61; Birge ratio 0.27 (not above one, so no widening); final width x/÷3.61.

Result: central **15,819** posting, 80% interval [4,385, 57,071] posting, basis blocks (several routes with separate inputs: the width is the typical width of the routes' own intervals, not narrowed for their number), weight rule equal_blocks_median, weight on methods that use assumptions 1.00

Unit of the estimate: posting (distinct job posting; total for 2026-01-01 to 2026-12-31)

## Published-value check (live use only)

Status: **no_candidates**. target period ends 2026-12-31, after as_of: it cannot have been published yet


## Final answer

The best estimate is 15,819 posting (80% model-based interval 4,385 to 57,071 posting) for distinct U.S. postings first published in 2026 for new-graduate summer internships and full-time entry-level professional roles whose text explicitly states visa sponsorship. Four routes were combined with equal weight per block: an all-posting flow route scaled by professional and new-grad shares and a sponsorship-intensity factor; a cap-registering employer route; an LCA route; and a direct JobShifu explicit-sponsorship count scaled to the LinkUp universe. Observed anchors include 4,960,000 job posting, 50.5 day, 0.18%, 57,600 employer, 541,620 certified filing, 2,673 job posting and 1,482,236 job posting. Everything else rests on analyst assumptions, notably the professional share 45%, new-grad share 10%, internship share 6%, intensity ratio 4, coverage ratios 0.7 and 0.8, and new-grad share of sponsored postings 0.25. The interval is conditional on these assumptions, not a demonstrated accuracy guarantee. The biggest risk is that explicit sponsorship language is rare and inconsistently detected, so the sponsorship rate and coverage factors could be off several-fold. Better evidence would be a large, deduplicated 2026 posting sample with verified sponsorship text and new-grad classification.

Unit of the estimate: posting (distinct job posting; total for 2026-01-01 to 2026-12-31).

Key assumptions (proposed by the analyst model, not observed; code samples each one between the bounds shown):
- a1 Professional white-collar share of all U.S. unique postings: 45%, range 30% to 60%; used by m1. Overturned by: Direct 2026 all-posting occupation tabulation showing professional white-collar share far outside [n]–[n].
- a2 Full-time new-grad [n]–[n] year share within professional postings: 10%, range 5% to 20%; used by m1. Overturned by: A 2026 professional-occupation tabulation showing the [n]–[n]-year new-grad share outside [n]–[n].
- a4 Summer internship share within professional postings: 6%, range 3% to 12%; used by m1. Overturned by: A direct 2026 professional posting sample showing internship share outside [n]–[n].
- a6 Relative explicit-sponsorship intensity of new-grad professional postings versus all U.S. postings: 4 ratio, range 1.5 to 10; used by m1. Overturned by: Direct 2026 new-grad professional text audit showing sponsorship intensity relative to all postings outside [n]–[n]x.
- a8 Share of FY[n] H-[n]B cap-registering employers with at least one qualifying explicitly sponsored new-grad/internship posting: 6%, range 2% to 15%; used by m2. Overturned by: Employer-site audit showing a much larger or smaller share of cap registrants with explicit new-grad sponsorship postings.
- a9 Average qualifying postings per advertising sponsor employer: 3 posting per employer, range 1 to 8; used by m2. Overturned by: A 2026 employer-level count of explicit new-grad sponsorship postings showing mean postings per advertising sponsor outside [n]–[n].
- a10 Share of LCA filings for new-grad [n]–[n] year professional roles: 3%, range 1% to 8%; used by m3. Overturned by: LCA wage-level/occupation tabulation for 2026 showing new-grad [n]–[n]-year share outside [n]–[n].
- a11 Ratio of distinct 2026 explicit-sponsorship new-grad professional postings to new-grad LCA filings: 0.8 posting per filing, range 0.3 to 2; used by m3. Overturned by: Matched employer audit showing postings per new-grad LCA far outside [n]–[n].
- a5 Coverage ratio of LCA-filing sponsor universe: qualifying postings by LCA-filing employers divided by all qualifying postings: 0.8 ratio, range 0.4873 to 0.9747 (range widened by code); used by m3. Overturned by: A matched audit showing that LCA filers account for far less than half of explicit new-grad sponsorship postings.
- a7 Coverage ratio of cap-registering sponsor universe: qualifying postings by FY[n] cap-registering employers divided by all qualifying postings: 0.7 ratio, range 0.4 to 0.9; used by m2. Overturned by: Employer audit showing cap registrants account for far less than [n] of explicit new-grad sponsorship postings.
- a12 Share of all explicit-sponsorship U.S. postings that are new-grad professional roles: 0.25 ratio, range 0.1 to 0.5; used by m4. Overturned by: A direct text audit of explicit-sponsorship postings showing the new-grad professional share outside [n]–[n].

Method details (computed by code):
- m1 formula_mc: (p75 * k_days_per_year / p81) * a1 * (a2 + a4) * p157 * a6; Monte Carlo P10 to P90 5,592 to 59,245; result unit (posting/year) * 1 year converted to posting; own 80% width x/÷3.26 (Monte Carlo of the formula inputs)
- m2 formula_mc: p259 * a8 * a9 / a7; Monte Carlo P10 to P90 2,097 to 42,586; result unit (0.05263157895 posting/month) * 1 year converted to posting; own 80% width x/÷4.51 (Monte Carlo of the formula inputs)
- m3 formula_mc: p368 * a10 * a11 / a5; Monte Carlo P10 to P90 3,943 to 73,945; result unit (posting/year) * 1 year converted to posting; own 80% width x/÷4.33 (Monte Carlo of the formula inputs)
- m4 formula_mc: (p154 * p75 / p153) * (k_days_per_year / p81) * a12; Monte Carlo P10 to P90 5,985 to 34,893; result unit (posting/year) * 1 year converted to posting; own 80% width x/÷2.41 (Monte Carlo of the formula inputs)
- Combination: 4 block(s), equal weight per block (methods whose inputs overlap by at least half form one block; backtest errors set widths, not weights): B1 = m1: 18,376 (width x/÷3.26, Monte Carlo of the formula inputs); B2 = m2: 9,413 (width x/÷4.51, Monte Carlo of the formula inputs); B3 = m3: 17,194 (width x/÷4.33, Monte Carlo of the formula inputs); B4 = m4: 14,554 (width x/÷2.41, Monte Carlo of the formula inputs). Estimate = the median of the blocks.
- Interval: root mean square of the block widths x/÷3.61; Birge ratio 0.27 (not above one, so no widening); final width x/÷3.61.
